13. On other hand, the learned counsel appearing for the accused officer argued that the house is ancestral property: that the PW 2 need of ownership certificate which is filed in the suit against the temple committee: that the house of PW 2 is going through the temple lands and that compound wall was erected around the land of temple. It is caused inconvenience to PW 2 to go to his house; that PW1 and 2 and said to have visited the Municipal office for issuance of ownership certificate; that on the said date the accused officer said to have been made a demand of bribe; but PW1 in his chief examination stated that he met on 2/1/2004 and that on the date of submission of application the accused officer was not available in the office; that Sri B. Satyanarayana was also stated the same in the post trap proceedings; that the accused officer and other staff members were on other duty hence the accused officer is not available:

<div style="text-align: center;"><img src="https://pplines-online.bj.bcebos.com/deploy/official/paddleocr/pp-ocr-vl-16-online//8aa46033-fdaa-4718-85f2-ac098988c358/markdown_0/imgs/img_in_seal_box_204_4454_995_5258.jpg?authorization=bce-auth-v1%2FALTAKDN8mY5KlNI7zaRpLmOqrw%2F2026-06-04T10%3A16%3A18Z%2F-1%2F%2F84ebc41d49be0d424764c4017834b4f800174e49ba3b3da87eef8ecaea318c01" alt="Image" width="23%" />  <div style="text-align: center;"><img src="https://pplines-online.bj.bcebos.com/deploy/official/paddleocr/pp-ocr-vl-16-online//8aa46033-fdaa-4718-85f2-ac098988c358/markdown_0/imgs/img_in_seal_box_204_4454_995_5258.jpg?authorization=bce-auth-v1%2FALTAKDN8mY5KlNI7zaRpLmOqrw%2F2026-06-04T10%3A16%3A18Z%2F-1%2F%2F84ebc41d49be0d424764c4017834b4f800174e49ba3b3da87eef8ecaea318c01" alt="Image" width="23%" />  proceedings; th were on other    </div>   </div>


CC No.42 of 2004

that PW 4 initialed Ex.P.1 application, that in Ex.D.1 which is the circular, the name of the accused officer and DW 1 are found; that Ex.D.1 circular was issued for attending election duty; that the cross examination of PW 2 shows that there was some difference with the accused officer; that the cross examination of PW1 and 2 shows the impression that the accused officer was instrumental for construction of compound wall around the temple land; that under section 13(1)(d) of Prevention of Corruption Act, the presumption is not available and that the prosecution failed to prove its case against the accused officer.

14. As seen from the evidence of PW 1 who is the son of PW 2 and owner of the house shows that PW 2 applied for issuance of house ownership certificate as the same was required to file in the civil suit. Admittedly Ex.P.1 application was filed on 2/1/2004 and that the trap was laid on 9/2/2004 ie. nearly one month seven days after submitting Ex.P.1 application. Therefore as on the date of trap incident, that Ex.P.1 presented by PW 2 was pending with the accused officer and the same was fully corroborated by the evidence of PW 1 and PW5. That Ex.P.1 application of PW 2 is pending since 2/1/2004 to till the date of trap incident i.e. on 9/2/2004. As seen from Ex.P.1 it was initialed by the concerned on 2/1/2004. PW 5 categorically

<div style="text-align: center;"><img src="https://pplines-online.bj.bcebos.com/deploy/official/paddleocr/pp-ocr-vl-16-online//8aa46033-fdaa-4718-85f2-ac098988c358/markdown_1/imgs/img_in_seal_box_175_4598_998_5371.jpg?authorization=bce-auth-v1%2FALTAKDN8mY5KlNI7zaRpLmOqrw%2F2026-06-04T10%3A16%3A20Z%2F-1%2F%2F8dc0c793b767ff172da200c86d32552c8f894f8d9cc8b4d6e8853954049c38e6" alt="Image" width="24%" />  <div style="text-align: center;"><img src="https://pplines-online.bj.bcebos.com/deploy/official/paddleocr/pp-ocr-vl-16-online//8aa46033-fdaa-4718-85f2-ac098988c358/markdown_1/imgs/img_in_seal_box_175_4598_998_5371.jpg?authorization=bce-auth-v1%2FALTAKDN8mY5KlNI7zaRpLmOqrw%2F2026-06-04T10%3A16%3A20Z%2F-1%2F%2F8dc0c793b767ff172da200c86d32552c8f894f8d9cc8b4d6e8853954049c38e6" alt="Image" width="24%" />  AOB  </div>   </div>


CC No.42 of 2004

deposed that on 2/1/2004 he endorsed on Ex.P.1 to revenue section and that the accused officer has to put up the file for issuance of ownership and that the accused officer did not put the file before him. PW 4 deposed that on 9/2/2004 at about 12.15 p.m. the accused officer came to his room and put up a note before him and on seeing the note he endorsed on the note chit that to collect the fee and issue certificate; that after five minutes the accused officer came to him with a further note informing that he (AO) collected Rs.100/- towards fee and he perused the note and also signed on the certificate. Therefore from the evidence of PW 4 and 5 coupled with the documentary evidence Ex.P.1 application is pending with the accused officer from 2/1/2004 till the date of trap incident. Only on the date of trap incident, when PW 1 met the accused officer and informed that he brought the bribe amount, then only the accused officer has put up the note and taken the signature of the concerned. Therefore it is very clear that the official favour is pending with the accused officer till the date of trap incident. Of course, the defence of the accused officer is that he (AO) was on election duty along with other staff members and filed Ex.D.1 proceedings issued by RDO Sanga Reddy dated 29/12/2003. On perusal of Ex.D.1 which is the circular issued by RDO wherein it is

<div style="text-align: center;"><img src="https://pplines-online.bj.bcebos.com/deploy/official/paddleocr/pp-ocr-vl-16-online//8aa46033-fdaa-4718-85f2-ac098988c358/markdown_2/imgs/img_in_seal_box_322_4620_1125_5119.jpg?authorization=bce-auth-v1%2FALTAKDN8mY5KlNI7zaRpLmOqrw%2F2026-06-04T10%3A16%3A23Z%2F-1%2F%2F367c468d219b544c4d3e39b4ffacdf837181673abeb2f2adc0027c18250cb184" alt="Image" width="23%" />  <div style="text-align: center;"><img src="https://pplines-online.bj.bcebos.com/deploy/official/paddleocr/pp-ocr-vl-16-online//8aa46033-fdaa-4718-85f2-ac098988c358/markdown_2/imgs/img_in_seal_box_322_4620_1125_5119.jpg?authorization=bce-auth-v1%2FALTAKDN8mY5KlNI7zaRpLmOqrw%2F2026-06-04T10%3A16%3A23Z%2F-1%2F%2F367c468d219b544c4d3e39b4ffacdf837181673abeb2f2adc0027c18250cb184" alt="Image" width="23%" />  C.O.I.C.O.D  </div>   </div>


CCNo.42of2004

mentioned that that the collector Medak has issued orders for formation of at least 10 teams in the municipal level with the staff available in revenue Municipal and panchyath raj departments for verification of claims received in form 6 and verification of electoral rolls to find out the dead, migrated voters in the voters list and residual voters list for qualifying the date as on 1/1/2004 and that the collector Medak will held the meeting with the team members on 29/12/2003 at 4.30 p.m. in the chambers of collector Medak in connection with the procedure to be following in verification of claims and objections. Therefore Ex.D.1 indicates that the teams formed for verification and meeting with the collector on 29/12/2003 to discuss the matter only. But it was indicated that the accused officer and along with other staff members were deputed for the same work from 2/1/2004 to 9/2/2004 and the same is also not reflected in the attendance register Ex.P.6 as the accused officer was on other duty from 2/1/2004 onwards. Therefore Ex.D.1 circular was issued only to conduct the meeting on 29/12/2003 with the collector Medak to discuss the matter. If any work is entrusted to the accused officer that work including the normal duty of the accused officer in the office. There are no specific orders are issued to the accused officer by deputing him for election duty.

<div style="text-align: center;"><img src="https://pplines-online.bj.bcebos.com/deploy/official/paddleocr/pp-ocr-vl-16-online//8aa46033-fdaa-4718-85f2-ac098988c358/markdown_3/imgs/img_in_seal_box_168_4433_964_5256.jpg?authorization=bce-auth-v1%2FALTAKDN8mY5KlNI7zaRpLmOqrw%2F2026-06-04T10%3A16%3A26Z%2F-1%2F%2Fdc762d686578ab2a127505bbdb4dea67765a0ff6d3dcaf0b1a474d62c4b1579f" alt="Image" width="23%" />  <div style="text-align: center;"><img src="https://pplines-online.bj.bcebos.com/deploy/official/paddleocr/pp-ocr-vl-16-online//8aa46033-fdaa-4718-85f2-ac098988c358/markdown_3/imgs/img_in_seal_box_168_4433_964_5256.jpg?authorization=bce-auth-v1%2FALTAKDN8mY5KlNI7zaRpLmOqrw%2F2026-06-04T10%3A16%3A26Z%2F-1%2F%2Fdc762d686578ab2a127505bbdb4dea67765a0ff6d3dcaf0b1a474d62c4b1579f" alt="Image" width="23%" />  4205  </div>   </div>


CC No. 42 of 2004

from 2/1/2004 till the date of trap incident. It is the bounden duty of the accused officer that in addition to the normal duty, he has to work the election duty if he is deputed for the work. The accused officer has signed in the attendance register from 2/1/2004 till the date of trap incident. If the accused officer was deputed wholly for election duty, his presence is mentioned as Other duty in the attendance. But he has signed in the attendance register from 2/1/2004 to till the date of trap incident, and AO has not done it intentionally for extracting money. Therefore official favour is pending with the accused officer by the date of trap incident.

15. With regard to the demand, it is the fact that as per Ex.P.2 complaint PW 1 did not mention the various dates of demand as stated by PW 1 in his evidence. In Ex.P.2 it is mentioned that to enquire the application made by his father on 2/1/2004 for issuance of house ownership certificate and when he met the accused officer, the accused officer demanded Rs.1000/- for process the application of his father. PW 1 did not specify the dates on Ex.P.2 application, but he met the accused officer two or three times. PW 1 clearly mentioned Ex.P.2 application that he met the accused officer and enquired with the accused officer about the application given by his father and that

<div style="text-align: center;"><img src="https://pplines-online.bj.bcebos.com/deploy/official/paddleocr/pp-ocr-vl-16-online//8aa46033-fdaa-4718-85f2-ac098988c358/markdown_4/imgs/img_in_seal_box_105_4338_896_5118.jpg?authorization=bce-auth-v1%2FALTAKDN8mY5KlNI7zaRpLmOqrw%2F2026-06-04T10%3A16%3A29Z%2F-1%2F%2F4c5e278fb46e5f8aa9a8f3c9e1962419f145a3d46eb6531533ba054f57e99b7b" alt="Image" width="23%" />  <div style="text-align: center;"><img src="https://pplines-online.bj.bcebos.com/deploy/official/paddleocr/pp-ocr-vl-16-online//8aa46033-fdaa-4718-85f2-ac098988c358/markdown_4/imgs/img_in_seal_box_105_4338_896_5118.jpg?authorization=bce-auth-v1%2FALTAKDN8mY5KlNI7zaRpLmOqrw%2F2026-06-04T10%3A16%3A29Z%2F-1%2F%2F4c5e278fb46e5f8aa9a8f3c9e1962419f145a3d46eb6531533ba054f57e99b7b" alt="Image" width="23%" />  C.S.C  </div>   </div>


CC No.42 of 2004

the accused officer demanded Rs.1000/ to process the application of his father. PW 1 also informed the same to his father about the demand made the accused officer for issuance of ownership certificate. PW 1 also clearly stated in 164 Cr.P.C. statement about the demand of bribe made by the accused officer. Therefore PW 1 who is no other than the son of PW 2 who has personally met the accused officer to enquire about the application made by his father for issuance of ownership certificate, then the accused officer demanded a bribe of Rs.1000/- for processing the application of PW 2. PW 1 made the complaint Ex.P.2 on 6/2/2004 to the ACB officials stating that the accused officer demanded Rs.1000/ for processing the application of PW 2 for issuance of ownership certificate. PW 1 also clinchingly stated in his 164 Cr.P.C. statement about the demand of bribe made by the accused officer. Therefore it is very clear from the evidence of PW 1 coupled with the contents of Ex.P.2 as well as 164 Cr.P.C. statement, the accused officer has demanded the bribe of Rs.1000/- for issuance of house ownership certificate to PW 1. The demand of bribe is only known to bribe given and bribe taken as it is secret action and it cannot be known to 3rd parties. Therefore the demand is made by the accused officer is established by the prosecution beyond

<div style="text-align: center;"><img src="https://pplines-online.bj.bcebos.com/deploy/official/paddleocr/pp-ocr-vl-16-online//c0aa88bc-161d-4d75-99c6-aea081d31e02/markdown_0/imgs/img_in_seal_box_512_4674_1314_5393.jpg?authorization=bce-auth-v1%2FALTAKDN8mY5KlNI7zaRpLmOqrw%2F2026-06-04T10%3A16%3A16Z%2F-1%2F%2F813b5d751bef49f3cb8f9b09f52fb14f56503be2d2274d2cfbb73418183e917b" alt="Image" width="23%" />  <div style="text-align: center;"><img src="https://pplines-online.bj.bcebos.com/deploy/official/paddleocr/pp-ocr-vl-16-online//c0aa88bc-161d-4d75-99c6-aea081d31e02/markdown_0/imgs/img_in_seal_box_512_4674_1314_5393.jpg?authorization=bce-auth-v1%2FALTAKDN8mY5KlNI7zaRpLmOqrw%2F2026-06-04T10%3A16%3A16Z%2F-1%2F%2F813b5d751bef49f3cb8f9b09f52fb14f56503be2d2274d2cfbb73418183e917b" alt="Image" width="23%" />  AOG CO.,LTD.  </div>   </div>


CC No. 42 of 2004

all reasonable doubts

16. The contention of the accused officer is that there is misconception of PWs 1 and 2 with the accused officer as the accused officer was the instrumental in grant of permission to Basveshwar temple committee for the construction of compound wall around the temple land which PWs 1 and 2 used to use as passage to go to their house. But it does not mean to effect the case of the prosecution since that act has to done and even if there is a possibility of instigation by the accused officer. It is no way concerned to the trap incident, since the application of PW 2 pending with the accused officer from 2/1/2004 to till the date of trap incident. Therefore the version of the defence has no force.

17. Coming to the acceptance, admittedly the accused officer has accepted the said amount, but his contention is that PW 1 thrusted the amount into his pant pocket. The accused officer has examined DWs 1 and 2 who are working in the office of the accused officer. DW 1 in his evidence stated that he was attached to the accused officer for the duties allotted to them in the proceedings. But in the cross examination DW 1 admitted that there is no documentary evidence to show that he along with others attended the electoral works during January and February. 2024 and that he was not with the accused officer.

<div style="text-align: center;"><img src="https://pplines-online.bj.bcebos.com/deploy/official/paddleocr/pp-ocr-vl-16-online//c0aa88bc-161d-4d75-99c6-aea081d31e02/markdown_1/imgs/img_in_seal_box_334_4557_1126_5262.jpg?authorization=bce-auth-v1%2FALTAKDN8mY5KlNI7zaRpLmOqrw%2F2026-06-04T10%3A16%3A19Z%2F-1%2F%2F089c19b725e378b3a34650cb1ed7f1dad39d923adfe9180fdd540fbade28211a" alt="Image" width="23%" />  <div style="text-align: center;"><img src="https://pplines-online.bj.bcebos.com/deploy/official/paddleocr/pp-ocr-vl-16-online//c0aa88bc-161d-4d75-99c6-aea081d31e02/markdown_1/imgs/img_in_seal_box_334_4557_1126_5262.jpg?authorization=bce-auth-v1%2FALTAKDN8mY5KlNI7zaRpLmOqrw%2F2026-06-04T10%3A16%3A19Z%2F-1%2F%2F089c19b725e378b3a34650cb1ed7f1dad39d923adfe9180fdd540fbade28211a" alt="Image" width="23%" />  February 20  </div>   </div>


That Ex P.6 attendance register did not disclose that himself and the accused officer attended to the electoral works for one and half months. Further no separate proceedings were issued to them to attend the electoral works. Therefore the evidence of DW 1 would not reveal that both himself and the accused officer attended the electoral works from 2/1/2004 to till the date of trap incident. Coming to the evidence of DW 2, DW 2 in his cross examination stated that he did not make any representation to DG ACB informing that PW 1 thrusted the amount into the pant pocket of the accused officer. That there are no disputes in between PW 1 and the accused officer. That he does not know what transpired in between PW 1 and the accused officer in connection with the processing of ownership certificate prior to 9/2/2004. Therefore the evidence of DWs 1 and 2 are no way helpful to the accused officer. The presence of DWs 1 and 2 are also not mentioned in the post trap proceedings.

18. Coming to the spontaneous explanation of the accused officer in Ex.P.8 post trap proceedings, the accused officer denied to have demanded any brthe from the complaint but admitted to have accepted the amount of Rs.1000/- from the complaint when offered by him voluntarily. It is the bounded duty of the accused officer how he was in possession of the tainted amount.

<div style="text-align: center;"><img src="https://pplines-online.bj.bcebos.com/deploy/official/paddleocr/pp-ocr-vl-16-online//c0aa88bc-161d-4d75-99c6-aea081d31e02/markdown_2/imgs/img_in_seal_box_1969_4604_2794_5022.jpg?authorization=bce-auth-v1%2FALTAKDN8mY5KlNI7zaRpLmOqrw%2F2026-06-04T10%3A16%3A22Z%2F-1%2F%2F53827c5d015dd56976599548b0e4bc477434b85e9fd807e724bdb0054d02409c" alt="Image" width="24%" />  <div style="text-align: center;"><img src="https://pplines-online.bj.bcebos.com/deploy/official/paddleocr/pp-ocr-vl-16-online//c0aa88bc-161d-4d75-99c6-aea081d31e02/markdown_2/imgs/img_in_seal_box_1969_4604_2794_5022.jpg?authorization=bce-auth-v1%2FALTAKDN8mY5KlNI7zaRpLmOqrw%2F2026-06-04T10%3A16%3A22Z%2F-1%2F%2F53827c5d015dd56976599548b0e4bc477434b85e9fd807e724bdb0054d02409c" alt="Image" width="24%" />  n;of the tainted amort  </div>   </div>


<div style="text-align: center;"><img src="https://pplines-online.bj.bcebos.com/deploy/official/paddleocr/pp-ocr-vl-16-online//c0aa88bc-161d-4d75-99c6-aea081d31e02/markdown_2/imgs/img_in_seal_box_408_4640_1193_5367.jpg?authorization=bce-auth-v1%2FALTAKDN8mY5KlNI7zaRpLmOqrw%2F2026-06-04T10%3A16%3A23Z%2F-1%2F%2Fd32857316c4cf993af25d9537282e01652616a49d20bc14e0aa0fe17005a077c" alt="Image" width="23%" />  <div style="text-align: center;"><img src="https://pplines-online.bj.bcebos.com/deploy/official/paddleocr/pp-ocr-vl-16-online//c0aa88bc-161d-4d75-99c6-aea081d31e02/markdown_2/imgs/img_in_seal_box_408_4640_1193_5367.jpg?authorization=bce-auth-v1%2FALTAKDN8mY5KlNI7zaRpLmOqrw%2F2026-06-04T10%3A16%3A23Z%2F-1%2F%2Fd32857316c4cf993af25d9537282e01652616a49d20bc14e0aa0fe17005a077c" alt="Image" width="23%" />  302  </div>   </div>


CC No. 42 of 2004

That the phenolphthalein test conducted on right hand fingers as well as inner linings of the pant pocket of the accused officer are yielded positive request when it was tested with chemicals. The description of the amount recovered from the accused officer at the time of post trap proceedings were also tallied with the description of the amount mentioned in the pre trap proceedings. It is the bounded duty of the accused officer to explain about the possession of the tainted amount. The contention of the defence is that one B. Satyanarayan was present at the time of post trap proceedings. But the prosecution has not examined the said Satyanarayana. The accused officer has also not examined the said Satyanarayana to disprove the case of the prosecution. Hence non examination of Satyanarayana by the prosecution is not fatal to the case of the prosecution as it may or may not examine the witnesses cited in the list. The names of DWS 1 and 2 are not find place in Ex.P.8 post trap proceedings. But the accused officer has examined them on his behalf. But their evidence is no way helpful to the accused officer.

19. The learned public prosecutor relied on the following decisions: He relied on a decision reported in 2004 Crl.L.J. 620] (

A.P. Vs Vasudeva Rao) wherein it was held that

No explanation offered for presence of phenolphthalein

<div style="text-align: center;"><img src="https://pplines-online.bj.bcebos.com/deploy/official/paddleocr/pp-ocr-vl-16-online//c0aa88bc-161d-4d75-99c6-aea081d31e02/markdown_3/imgs/img_in_seal_box_271_4430_1066_5268.jpg?authorization=bce-auth-v1%2FALTAKDN8mY5KlNI7zaRpLmOqrw%2F2026-06-04T10%3A16%3A26Z%2F-1%2F%2F408818a282deb77f78bc4d8110117a02ed5551b730cfd8a2b7f503cd36762f32" alt="Image" width="23%" />  <div style="text-align: center;"><img src="https://pplines-online.bj.bcebos.com/deploy/official/paddleocr/pp-ocr-vl-16-online//c0aa88bc-161d-4d75-99c6-aea081d31e02/markdown_3/imgs/img_in_seal_box_271_4430_1066_5268.jpg?authorization=bce-auth-v1%2FALTAKDN8mY5KlNI7zaRpLmOqrw%2F2026-06-04T10%3A16%3A26Z%2F-1%2F%2F408818a282deb77f78bc4d8110117a02ed5551b730cfd8a2b7f503cd36762f32" alt="Image" width="23%" />  C.A.P.V  </div>   </div>


CC No.42 of 2004

powder and that when receipt of money was admitted it

was for accused to prove as to show the presumption

under section 4 of the Act is not available.

in the instant case on hand, the accused officer has not taken the plea of thrusting theory at the time of spontaneous explanation in the post trap proceedings with regard to the tainted amount and that the tainted amount was recovered from his possession. Therefore the above decision is rightly applicable to the facts of the case on hand.

He also relied on a decision reported in 2000(1) Crimes

79 SC ( M.Narstinga Rao Vs. State of A.P.) wherein it was held

that

Presumption as to acceptance of gratification.

compulsory presumption, scope and ambition of shall

presume meaning of can a legal presumption he based

on a factual presumption, to draw presumption under

section 20 prosecution need not establish with direct

evidence that delinquent public servant accepted or

obtained gratification.

In the instant case the thrusting theory set up by the accused

officer is fully rejected and no way helpful to him. The accused

officer has taken inconsistent pleas with regard to tainted

<div style="text-align: center;"><img src="https://pplines-online.bj.bcebos.com/deploy/official/paddleocr/pp-ocr-vl-16-online//c0aa88bc-161d-4d75-99c6-aea081d31e02/markdown_4/imgs/img_in_seal_box_474_4635_1278_5343.jpg?authorization=bce-auth-v1%2FALTAKDN8mY5KlNI7zaRpLmOqrw%2F2026-06-04T10%3A16%3A29Z%2F-1%2F%2Fbff180633d3a8c900837ad77f5709f7ada71580c4320192503345b28fdbcd612" alt="Image" width="23%" />  <div style="text-align: center;"><img src="https://pplines-online.bj.bcebos.com/deploy/official/paddleocr/pp-ocr-vl-16-online//c0aa88bc-161d-4d75-99c6-aea081d31e02/markdown_4/imgs/img_in_seal_box_474_4635_1278_5343.jpg?authorization=bce-auth-v1%2FALTAKDN8mY5KlNI7zaRpLmOqrw%2F2026-06-04T10%3A16%3A29Z%2F-1%2F%2Fbff180633d3a8c900837ad77f5709f7ada71580c4320192503345b28fdbcd612" alt="Image" width="23%" />  A.C.E.  </div>   </div>


<div style="text-align: center;"><img src="https://pplines-online.bj.bcebos.com/deploy/official/paddleocr/pp-ocr-vl-16-online//c0aa88bc-161d-4d75-99c6-aea081d31e02/markdown_4/imgs/img_in_seal_box_2917_4709_3065_5045.jpg?authorization=bce-auth-v1%2FALTAKDN8mY5KlNI7zaRpLmOqrw%2F2026-06-04T10%3A16%3A30Z%2F-1%2F%2F47687c8e6015281eab0ca7a6677c038d05e0aad7ae34f74adabfbc43392653d3" alt="Image" width="4%" />  <div style="text-align: center;"><img src="https://pplines-online.bj.bcebos.com/deploy/official/paddleocr/pp-ocr-vl-16-online//c0aa88bc-161d-4d75-99c6-aea081d31e02/markdown_4/imgs/img_in_seal_box_2917_4709_3065_5045.jpg?authorization=bce-auth-v1%2FALTAKDN8mY5KlNI7zaRpLmOqrw%2F2026-06-04T10%3A16%3A30Z%2F-1%2F%2F47687c8e6015281eab0ca7a6677c038d05e0aad7ae34f74adabfbc43392653d3" alt="Image" width="4%" />  中国银行股份有限公司  </div>   </div>


CC No. 42 of 2004

amount. The explanation made by the accused officer is only an
after thought to rescue from this case. Hence the above decision
is also rightly applicable to the facts of the case on hand.

He relied on a decision reported in 2004 Crl.L.J. 884 (T.Shankar Prasad Vs. State of A.P.) wherein it was held that:

Illegal gratification, acceptance of by a public servant.
presumption under section 20 of PC Act, 1988 is a
presumption of law and case an obligation on court to
operate it in every case brought in. It is rebutted by prof
and not be explanation which may seem to be plausible.
Here in this case, the tainted amount is recovered from the
possession of the accused officer and the chemical test is also
yielded positive result. The accused officer has to explain by way
of proof but not by mere an explanation. Of course the accused
officer has examined DW land 2, but their evidence is no way
helpful to the accused officer, as the work of PW 1 is pending
with the accused officer by the date of trap incident. Therefore
the above said decision is quite applicable to the facts of the
present case.

He also relied on a decision reported in 2006(1) SCC

(Kanshiram Vs. State of Punjab) wherein it was

<div style="text-align: center;"><img src="https://pplines-online.bj.bcebos.com/deploy/official/paddleocr/pp-ocr-vl-16-online//79370212-ceee-4606-a494-ee6dcf890dfd/markdown_0/imgs/img_in_seal_box_294_4437_1094_5267.jpg?authorization=bce-auth-v1%2FALTAKDN8mY5KlNI7zaRpLmOqrw%2F2026-06-04T10%3A17%3A05Z%2F-1%2F%2F5e999ad953bb8fe05239c57e5a5569578a9a444a7911b8e6f32410a256a3af08" alt="Image" width="23%" />  <div style="text-align: center;"><img src="https://pplines-online.bj.bcebos.com/deploy/official/paddleocr/pp-ocr-vl-16-online//79370212-ceee-4606-a494-ee6dcf890dfd/markdown_0/imgs/img_in_seal_box_294_4437_1094_5267.jpg?authorization=bce-auth-v1%2FALTAKDN8mY5KlNI7zaRpLmOqrw%2F2026-06-04T10%3A17%3A05Z%2F-1%2F%2F5e999ad953bb8fe05239c57e5a5569578a9a444a7911b8e6f32410a256a3af08" alt="Image" width="23%" />  CTO5T59P598 held that;  </div>   </div>


CC No.42 of 2004

Receipt of money proved beyond reasonable doubts, no reasonable explanation offered by by the accused officer as to how to the tainted amount came to his possession held it would be presumed under section 20 that the accused accepted the bribe on facts the explanation offered by the appellant was not sufficient to rebut the presumption.

In the instant case the accused officer has not placed any rebuttal evidence to show that how he is in possession of the tainted amount. Therefore the above decision is rightly applicable to the facts of the case on hand.

Finally he also relied on a decision reported in 2004(2)

Supreme 70 ( State of AP  Vs.  C. Uma Maheshwara Rao ) wherein

it was held that:

Presumption where public servant accepted gratification

other than legal remuneration, expression shall be

presumed employed in section 20 of the Act must have

import of compulsion, receipt and recovery of money

was accepted and High court itself had found plea of

loan not cogent or credible, expression gratification

when receipt was admitted it was for accused to prove

as to how presumption was not available as per force

<div style="text-align: center;"><img src="https://pplines-online.bj.bcebos.com/deploy/official/paddleocr/pp-ocr-vl-16-online//79370212-ceee-4606-a494-ee6dcf890dfd/markdown_1/imgs/img_in_seal_box_17_4427_843_5267.jpg?authorization=bce-auth-v1%2FALTAKDN8mY5KlNI7zaRpLmOqrw%2F2026-06-04T10%3A17%3A09Z%2F-1%2F%2Fbaa07f6cfb1764ded6e0c99d93c76978cd179ac9f950334c959a200a8d229c9f" alt="Image" width="24%" />  <div style="text-align: center;"><img src="https://pplines-online.bj.bcebos.com/deploy/official/paddleocr/pp-ocr-vl-16-online//79370212-ceee-4606-a494-ee6dcf890dfd/markdown_1/imgs/img_in_seal_box_17_4427_843_5267.jpg?authorization=bce-auth-v1%2FALTAKDN8mY5KlNI7zaRpLmOqrw%2F2026-06-04T10%3A17%3A09Z%2F-1%2F%2Fbaa07f6cfb1764ded6e0c99d93c76978cd179ac9f950334c959a200a8d229c9f" alt="Image" width="24%" />  广西壮族自治区 品监督管理局  </div>   </div>


CC No. 42 of 2004

the presumption arises and becomes operative, high

court was not justified in directing acquittal and it was

liable to be set aside.

In the instant case the thrusting theory set up by the accused officer is totally rejected; that the explanation offered by the accused officer is inconsistent and he failed to explain how the tainted amount was came into his possession and the version of thrusting theory set up by the accused officer is totally denied by PW 1. Hence the above said decision is well applicable to the facts of the case on hand.

20. In view of the above discussion, it is proved that the official favour is pending with the accused officer; that the demand and recovery of the tainted amount is also proved by the evidence of PW 1, 3 and 6. On the other hand, the accused officer did not dispute about the receipt of the tainted amount and recovery of the same from his pant pocket at his instance. But the contention of the accused officer that PW 1 thrusted the amount into his pant pocket. The said plea of the accused officer cannot be accepted as the said version is after thought only. Therefore the case of the prosecution is proved by the evidence of

3 and 6. The evidence of DW land 2 is no way helpful to

based officer. Considering the above said decisions relied

<div style="text-align: center;"><img src="https://pplines-online.bj.bcebos.com/deploy/official/paddleocr/pp-ocr-vl-16-online//79370212-ceee-4606-a494-ee6dcf890dfd/markdown_2/imgs/img_in_seal_box_218_4456_1011_5324.jpg?authorization=bce-auth-v1%2FALTAKDN8mY5KlNI7zaRpLmOqrw%2F2026-06-04T10%3A17%3A12Z%2F-1%2F%2F1298046040833c61f2cf13d1cc00e71792f691df6a28f9e55dd726e7b525c6a1" alt="Image" width="23%" />  <div style="text-align: center;"><img src="https://pplines-online.bj.bcebos.com/deploy/official/paddleocr/pp-ocr-vl-16-online//79370212-ceee-4606-a494-ee6dcf890dfd/markdown_2/imgs/img_in_seal_box_218_4456_1011_5324.jpg?authorization=bce-auth-v1%2FALTAKDN8mY5KlNI7zaRpLmOqrw%2F2026-06-04T10%3A17%3A12Z%2F-1%2F%2F1298046040833c61f2cf13d1cc00e71792f691df6a28f9e55dd726e7b525c6a1" alt="Image" width="23%" />  PW.1.3 anc 000000   </div>   </div>


CC No.42 of 200

on by the prosecution after discussing in detail above, the

prosecution has proved its case beyond reasonable doubts.

21. After summing up the entire evidence and in view of my discussions with regard to principles of law, apart from the legal position relied on by the special public prosecutor, it is a clear case of public servant taking gratification other than legal remuneration in respect of official act or favour to do any favour to PW1. The accused officer being the public servant committed the offence of misconduct having accepted the bribe amount for themselves other than legal remuneration. The accused officer received the tainted amount from PW1. Therefore the prosecution has established the offences with which the accused officer was charged beyond reasonable doubts. Accordingly these points are answered in favour of the prosecution as against the accused officer.

22. POINT NO 5: In view of my discussions at point No.1 to

4. the prosecution has proved the charges framed against the

officer beyond reasonable doubt. Accordingly this point is also

answered in favour of the prospection as against the accused

officer.

23. IN THE RESULT, the accused officer is found guilty of the charges framed against him under section 7 and section

<div style="text-align: center;"><img src="https://pplines-online.bj.bcebos.com/deploy/official/paddleocr/pp-ocr-vl-16-online//79370212-ceee-4606-a494-ee6dcf890dfd/markdown_3/imgs/img_in_seal_box_175_4623_986_5392.jpg?authorization=bce-auth-v1%2FALTAKDN8mY5KlNI7zaRpLmOqrw%2F2026-06-04T10%3A17%3A16Z%2F-1%2F%2F01f4e464f9539ba506b7706cb01e21d28b7d310b2941bf66cf98527532dea871" alt="Image" width="23%" />  <div style="text-align: center;"><img src="https://pplines-online.bj.bcebos.com/deploy/official/paddleocr/pp-ocr-vl-16-online//79370212-ceee-4606-a494-ee6dcf890dfd/markdown_3/imgs/img_in_seal_box_175_4623_986_5392.jpg?authorization=bce-auth-v1%2FALTAKDN8mY5KlNI7zaRpLmOqrw%2F2026-06-04T10%3A17%3A16Z%2F-1%2F%2F01f4e464f9539ba506b7706cb01e21d28b7d310b2941bf66cf98527532dea871" alt="Image" width="23%" />  SPEATA CO  </div>   </div>


CC No. 42 of 2004

13(1)(d) r/w sec. 13(2) of Prevention of Corruption Act, 1988 and

that he is convicted under section 248(2) Cr.P.C.

24. The accused officer is questioned about the quantum of the sentence. He stated that he is only earning member in the family; that there is no other person to look after his family; that he is having wife and children; that he is also having old aged mother of 80 years and prayed for mercy.

25. Having regard to the facts and circumstances of the case and submission of the accused officer, I herewith award the following sentences to the accused officer:

The accused officer is sentenced to undergo R.I. for period of 6 months and to pay a fine of Rs.500/- in default to undergo S.I. for a period of one month for the offence punishable under section 7 of the Prevention of corruption Act.

1988.

Further the accused officer is further sentenced to under go R.I. for a period of one year and to pay a fine of Rs.500/- in default to under go S.I. for a period for one months for the offence punishable under section 13(1)(d) r/w sec.13(2) of

Prevention of Corruption Act, 1988.

<div style="text-align: center;"><img src="https://pplines-online.bj.bcebos.com/deploy/official/paddleocr/pp-ocr-vl-16-online//79370212-ceee-4606-a494-ee6dcf890dfd/markdown_4/imgs/img_in_seal_box_247_4242_1023_5076.jpg?authorization=bce-auth-v1%2FALTAKDN8mY5KlNI7zaRpLmOqrw%2F2026-06-04T10%3A17%3A19Z%2F-1%2F%2F1866bd5fd3d2db41cdbeffd17c702b759bd1d48759e226bd46ea311da76e0873" alt="Image" width="22%" />  <div style="text-align: center;"><img src="https://pplines-online.bj.bcebos.com/deploy/official/paddleocr/pp-ocr-vl-16-online//79370212-ceee-4606-a494-ee6dcf890dfd/markdown_4/imgs/img_in_seal_box_247_4242_1023_5076.jpg?authorization=bce-auth-v1%2FALTAKDN8mY5KlNI7zaRpLmOqrw%2F2026-06-04T10%3A17%3A19Z%2F-1%2F%2F1866bd5fd3d2db41cdbeffd17c702b759bd1d48759e226bd46ea311da76e0873" alt="Image" width="22%" />  SPX officer sh  </div>   </div>


CC No. 42 of 2000

The remand period if any, shall be given set off U/s. 428

Cr.P.C.

MO 1 tainted amount of Rs.1000/- shall be returned to

PW 1 and that MOs 2 to 6 shall be destroyed after appeal time is

over.

Dictated to P.A. after transcribed by him, corrected and pronounced by me in open court, this the 13th day of October, 2008.

Mao

PRESS NOTE NO: 295

DATE: 21-10-2008

#### REVENUE OFFICIAL DISMISSED FROM SERVICE IN A.C.B. TRAP CASE

Sri Syed Omar Pasha, former Typist-cum Junior Assistant, Civil Supplies section incharge, R.D.Os Office, Sangareddy, Medak district has been dismissed from service by the Government of Andhra Pradesh on the report of the Tribunal for Disciplinary Proceedings, A.P. Hyderabad.

Earlier, the said Typist-cum-Junior Assistant was trapped by officials of the Anti-Corruption Bureau on 14.11.2002 when he demanded and accepted a bribe of Rs. 1,000/- from the complainant for showing official favour to process the file of temporary appointment of fair price shop dealership at Garlapally village. After completion of the investigation, the Anti-Corruption Bureau recommended to Government to place the above Accused Officer on his defence before the Tribunal for Disciplinary Proceedings.

<div style="text-align: center;"><img src="https://pplines-online.bj.bcebos.com/deploy/official/paddleocr/pp-ocr-vl-16-online//10c58c5a-8a70-4123-9554-4330d4d9358a/markdown_2/imgs/img_in_image_box_384_1975_651_2249.jpg?authorization=bce-auth-v1%2FALTAKDN8mY5KlNI7zaRpLmOqrw%2F2026-06-04T10%3A16%3A44Z%2F-1%2F%2F19816685bd3888363c0f9b893dc461af2d18733e83efbbae8c57fbb6a74a001a" alt="Image" width="7%" /></div>


##### JUNIOR ASSISTANT CONVICTED IN A.C.B. TRAP CASE

The Special Judge for S.P.E. & A.C.B. Cases, Hyderabad convicted Sri M. Janardhan, former Junior Assistant, Office of the Municipal Commissioner, Municipal Council, Sadasivpet, Medak district on 13.10.2008 and sentenced him to undergo six months rigorous imprisonment and to pay a fine of Rs. 500/- and in default, to suffer one month simple imprisonment for the offence punishable under section 7 of Prevention of Corruption Act, 1988. He further sentenced the Accused Officer to undergo rigorous imprisonment for 1 year and pay a fine of Rs. 500/- u/s 13(1)(d) of Prevention of Corruption Act, 1988, in default of payment of fine, to suffer one month simple imprisonment. Both the sentences of imprisonment shall run concurrently.

Earlier, he was trapped on 9.2.2004 by the Officials of the A.C.B. when he demanded and accepted a bribe amount of Rs. 1,000/- from the complainant for doing an official favour of processing his father's application for Ownership Certificate.

<div style="text-align: center;"><img src="https://pplines-online.bj.bcebos.com/deploy/official/paddleocr/pp-ocr-vl-16-online//10c58c5a-8a70-4123-9554-4330d4d9358a/markdown_2/imgs/img_in_image_box_448_3765_614_3922.jpg?authorization=bce-auth-v1%2FALTAKDN8mY5KlNI7zaRpLmOqrw%2F2026-06-04T10%3A16%3A45Z%2F-1%2F%2Fb4dd5000ead71c41fbcc7df0af796281d3ceca659d70030401d00cc51c123345" alt="Image" width="4%" /></div>


for Director General 21.10.08

Anti-Corruption Bureau,

A.P. HYDERABAD

From:

The Director General.

Anti-Corruption Bureau,

A.P., HYDERABAD.

To

The Principal Secretary to Government,

Municipal Administration & U.D. Department,

A.P., HYDERABAD.

Lr. No. 50/RCT-NMD/2004, date25-10-2008

Sir.

Sub:- Public Servants -- M.A.&U.D. Department-- Allegations of corruption against Sri Mamidi Janardhan, former Junior Assistant of Sadasivapet Municipality, Medak district -- Trapped on 9-2-2004 - Disposed by lower Court—A.O. convicted—Dismissal of A.O. from service—Requested-- Reg.

Ref: 1. This office final report of even No. dated 18-5-2004.

2. Govt. Memo. N. 3890/Vig. IV. 2/2004-2, dated 26-6-2004.

3. Lr. No. 84/VC-D1/04-3, dated 21-9-2004 of VC, AP., Hyderabad.

4. This office Lr. of even No. dated 20-1-2005.

Please refer to the correspondence cited.

The Principal Special Judge for SPE&ACB cases, Hyderabad pronounced judgement on 13-10-2008 in C.C.No.42/2004 convicting Sri M.Janardhan, former Junior Assistant of Sadasivpet Municipality, Medak district to undergo rigorous imprisonment for six months and to pay a fine of Rs.500/- and in default, to suffer one month, simple imprisonment for the offence under section 7 of Prevention of Corruption Act 1988 and he further sentenced the Accused Officer to undergo rigorous imprisonment for 1 year and pay a fine of Rs.500/- under section 13(2) r/w (1) (d) of Prevention of Corruption Act, 1988, in default of payment of fine, to suffer one month simple imprisonment. Both the sentences of imprisonment shall run concurrently. Copy of the judgement is enclosed herewith.

In G.O.Ms.NO.2 G.A.(Ser.C)Dept., dated 4-1-1999 Government directed that in all proven cases of misappropriation, bribery, corruption etc; the penalty of dismissal from service shall be imposed.

Hence, it is requested that Sri M. Janardhan, former Junior Assistant may be dismissed from service immediately on the ground of conduct which has led to his conviction on a criminal charge under rules 25(1) of the A.P.C.S. (CC.&A) Rules, 1991 without any notice.

As per the amendment of Rules 25(i) to the A.P.C.S.(CC.A) Rules, 1991 issued through G.O.Ms. No.240, G.A.(Ser.C)Dept., dated 14-6-2003, it is not necessary to consult with the A.P.P.S.C. before any orders are made under Clause (i) of this Rule.

A copy of the dismissal orders of Accused Officer Sri M. Janardhan may please be furnished to this Bureau for record.

Yours faithfully,

Encl: As above.

for Director General.

Anti-Corruption Bureau,

A.P., Hyderabad.

Public Services - M.A.Deptt - Sadasivpet - Sri M.Janardhan, Jr.Assistant

Sadasivpet - Trapped by ACB on 09.09.2004 - Suspension from Service - Revocation of Suspension - Prosecution sanctioned - Disposed by Lower Court - A.O. Convicted - Dismissal of A.O from service - Certain information - Submitted - Reg.

1. Govt. Memo. No. 3890/Vig. IV. 2/2004-5, Dt. 14.11.2006.

2. PROGS. ROC. No. 34/2004-A2, Dt. 3011.2006 of the RDMA, Hyd.

3. Lr. No. 50/RCT-NMD/2004, Dt. 25.10.2008 of the DG, ACB, A.P., Hyderabad addressed to Government & copy marked to this office.

I invite kind attention of the Government to the references cited. In the reference third cited, the Director General, Anti Corruption Bureau, A.P., Hyderabad has informed to Government that, "the Principal Special Judge for SPE & AC B cases, Hyderabad pronounced judgement on 13.10.2008 in C.C.No.42/2004 convicting Sri M.Janardhan, former Junior Assistant of Sadasivpet Municipality, Medak District to undergo rigorous imprisonment for six months and to pay a fine of Rs.500/- and in default, to suffer one month, simple imprisonment for the offence under section 7 of Prevention of Corruption Act, 1988 and he further sentenced the Accused Officer to undergo rigorous imprisonment for 1 year and pay a fine of Rs.500/- under section 13 (2) r/w (1) (d) of Prevention of Corruption Act, 1988, in default of payment of fine, to suffer one month simple imprisonment. Both the sentences of imprisonment shall run concurrently".

2. Further, the Director General, Anti Corruption Bureau, A.P., Hyderabad has informed that, "In G.O.Ms.No.2 G.A (Ser.C) Dept., Dt.04.01.1999 Government directed that in all proven cases of misappropriation, bribery, corruption etc; the penalty of dismissal from service shall be imposed and requested that Sri M.Janardhan, former Junior Assistant of Sadasivpet Municipality, Medak District may be dismissed from service immediately on the ground of conduct which has led to his conviction on a criminal charge under Rule 25 (1) of the A.P.C.S (CC&A) Rules, 1991 without any notice. Also, as per the amendments of Rule 25 (i) of the A.P.C.S (CC&A) Rules, 1991 issued through G.O.Ms.No.240 G.A (Ser.C) Dept., dated 14.06.2003, it is not necessary to consult with the A.P.P.S.C before any orders are made under clause (i) of this Rule".

3. In view of the above, I request the Government to issue necessary orders in the matter.

Yours faithfully,

Sd/- K.SHIVA PRASAD

ADDITIONAL DIRECTOR

Copy submitted to the Director General, Anti Corruption Bureau, A.P., Hyderabad.

Copy to the Regional Director-Cum-Appellate Commissioner of Municipal Administration, Hyderabad.

##### BRIEF FACTS OF THE CASE :-

On 09-02-2004 a trap was laid successfully against Sri. M. Janardhan, Junior Assistant, O/o Municipal Commissioner, Sadasivapeta, Medak District in Cr.No. 5/ACB-NZB/04 U/s 7 of P.C.Act, 1988 which was registered and investigated by Rewardee No 1.

Wherein the Rewardee 1 successfully laid a trap against the Accused Officer and conducted investigation in the above case. After receipt of sanctioned order from the Government Accordingly charge sheet was filed before Pri. Sp. Judge for SPE & ACB Cases, Hyderabad against Sri. M. Janardhan.

During the course of trail Rewardee 2 with the assistance of Rewardees 3 to 5 helped the SpI. P.P in conducting prosecution by producing the witnesses in time and briefing them in detail and in completing the prosecution evidence, which resulted in the successful prosecution of the case.

The Pri.Spl.Judge for SPE & ACB Cases, Hyderabad pronounced judgment in this case on 13-10-2008 convicting the Accused Officer Sri. M. Janardhan, Junior Assistant, O/o Municipal Commissioner, Sadasivapeta, Medak District and sentencing him to undergo R.I for six months and pay a fine of Rs. 5,000/- for the offence U/s 7 of Prevention of

corruption Act, 1988 and also under go R.I for 1 year and pay a fine of Rs. 500/- under section 13(1)(d) of Prevention of Corruption Act, 1988 and in default of payment of fine the Accused Officer to suffer one months simple imprisonment Both sentences of imprisonment shall run concurrently.

All the Rewardees who have worked with dedication need to be rewarded suitably for the good work done.

Therefore, it recommended sanctioning rewards mentioned against the Rewardees

 $ h $

Sri N. Sanyasi Rao, Prl., Special Judge for SPE & ACB Cases, Hyderabad pronounced judgment in the reference mentioned case today i.e. on 13-10-2008 convicting Accused Officer Sri M. Janardhan, Junior Assistant O/o Municipal commissioner, Municipal Council, Sadasiva Pet, Medak District. The Hon'ble Judge sentenced the Accused Officer to under go R.I. for Six Months and pay a fine of Rs.5,00/- for the offence U/s. 7 of Prevention of Corruption Act, 1988 and also under go R.I. for 1 year and pay a fine of Rs.500/- Under Section 13(1)(d) of prevention of Corruption Act, 1988, in default of payment of fine the Accused Officer to suffer one months Simple imprisonment Both sentences of imprisonment shall run concurrently.

Memo filed in the Honourable court for grant of free copy of judgment and on receipt of the same it would be forwarded to the Head Office.

Submitted for favour of information.

The said Appeal came-up for admission before the Hon'ble High Court on 10-11-08. The Hon'ble High Court while admitting the said Criminal appeal, granted bail to A.O., pending disposal of the said Criminal Appeal, vide Crl.A.M.P.No:2083/2008. I am herewith furnishing the said order copy.

# IN THE HIGH COURT OF JUDICATURE OF ANDHRA PRADESH AT HYDERABAD MONDAY THE TENTH DAY OF NOVEMBER, TWO THOUSAND AND EIGHT : PRESENT :

THE HONOURABLE SRI JUSTICE GOPALA KRISHNA TAMADA

CRLA.M.P. No. 2083 of 2008

IN

CRL.A. No. 1345 of 2008

Between:-

Sri Mamidi Janardhan S/o. Narsimhulu.

..Petitioner/Accused

(Appellant in Crl. A. No. 1345 of 2008

on the file of the High Court)

State of A.P., Rep. by Inspector of Police, Anti Corruption Bureau, through Special Public Prosecutor for A.C.B. Cases, High Court of A.P., Hyderabad.

COUNSEL FOR PETITIONER COUNSEL FOR RESPONDENT

..Respondent

(Respondent in-do-)

Sri N. Vijender Reddy

Sri V. Ravi Kiran Rao-SC

Petition under Section 389(1) of Cr.P.C. praying that in the circumstances stated in the Memo. of Grounds filed in Crl.A., the High Court may be pleased to enlarge the Accused on bail by suspending the sentence recorded in Judgment dt. 13-10-2008 in C.C.No. 42 of 2004 on the file of the Principal Special Judge for SPE & ACB Cases, City Civil Court, Hyderabad pending Crl.A.NO. 1345 of 2008 preferred there from to the High Court. The Court made the following

"Execution of sentence of imprisonment imposed against the petitioner/accused alone is suspended.

The petitioner shall be enlarged on bail on his executing a bond for a sum of RS.10,000/- (Rupees Ten thousand only) with two sureties of the like sum each to the satisfaction of the Principal Sessions Judge for SPE & ACB Cases, City Civil Court, Hyderabad.

SD/- V.T.V. KRISHNA RAO ASST. REGISTRAR

# ANDHRA PRADESH VIGILANCE COMMISSION SECRETARIAT, HYDERABAD.

Lr.No:84/VCD1/2004-5, Dated: 27-11-2008.

From

SRI K. SWAMINATHAN, IAS(Retd.),

Vigilance Commissioner.

To

10

The Principal Secy. to Government,

M.A.& U.D.Dept,

Secretariat, Hyderabad.

Sir,

Sub: P.S. – Allegation of corruption against Sri Mamidi Janardhan, Jr. Asst.

O/o Municipal Commr., Sadasivpet, Medak District– Trapped on

9.2.2004 – Charge sheet filed – Convicted – Reg.

<div style="text-align: center;"><img src="https://pplines-online.bj.bcebos.com/deploy/official/paddleocr/pp-ocr-vl-16-online//9debb0c7-94a1-41ae-88c6-bed05a7fce01/markdown_3/imgs/img_in_image_box_520_3207_719_3555.jpg?authorization=bce-auth-v1%2FALTAKDN8mY5KlNI7zaRpLmOqrw%2F2026-06-04T10%3A16%3A27Z%2F-1%2F%2Fc48fc132ae888056e11b8e31dc3574171f8d83d95cd3c06caafd6c407689f982" alt="Image" width="5%" /></div>


Ref: From D.G., ACB, Lr.No.50/RCT-NMD/04, Dt.25-10-2008.

###

Commission invites attention to the references cited.

2. Sri M. Janardhan, former Jr. Asst. of Sadasivapet Municipality, Medak Dist has been convicted by the Prl. Spl. Judge for SPE & ACB cases, Hyderabad under sections 7 & 13 (2) read with section 13 (1)(d) of the P.C. Act 1988 and sentenced the A.O to undergo rigorous imprisonment for 1 year and pay a fine of Rs. 500/-, in default of payment of fine, to suffer one month simple imprisonment. Both the sentences of imprisonment shall run concurrently.

3 As per clause (i) of rule 25 of the APCS (CCA) Rules, 1991, a penalty can be imposed on the ground of conduct leading to conviction on criminal charge without having to follow the procedure laid down under rules 20,21 & 22 thereof.

 $ x_{1} $

4. Such action should be taken without waiting for filing of appeal or the outcome of appeal if any filed and suspension of sentence is no bar to the taking of the action.

5. The conduct leading to the conviction on the criminal charge constitutes, on the part of Sri M. Janardhan, former Jr. Asst., grave misconduct of demanding and obtaining of corruption and bribery.

6. The penalty that should be imposed in a case of proven bribery and corruption as per the first proviso of rule 9 of the APCS (CCA) Rules, 1991 is dismissal or removal from service. G.O.Ms.No.2 GA(Ser.C) Dept.

Dt. 4-1-99, stipulates dismissal as the penalty in proven cases of misappropriation, bribery, bigamy, corruption, moral turpitude, forgery and outraging the modesty of a women etc. Further it shall not be necessary to consult APPSC for taking any such action.

7. The misconduct thus established against Sri M. Janardhan by his conviction on a criminal charge is grave and calls for imposition of the penalty of dismissal.

8. The competent authority may consider the facts of the case and impose the penalty of dismissal, in terms of clause (i) of rule 25 of the APCS (CCA) Rules, 1991.

9. Commission also requests the department to intimate the stage of departmental action against Sri M. Janardhan J.A., with regard to violation of conduct rules and failure to submit Annual Property Statements to the Department.

10. Receipt of the letter may please be acknowledged and action taken in the matter may be intimated.

Yours faithfully,

P. Kehrommbon 04112108

For VIGILANCE COMMISSIONER

(P. Krishnamacharyulu)

Asst. Secretary to V.C

Copy to:- The D.G., ACB,Hyd

From:
The Director General,
Anti-Corruption Bureau,
A.P., HYDERABAD.

To
The Principal Secretary to Government,
Municipal Administration & U.D. Department,
A.P., HYDERABAD.

Lr.No. 50/RCT-NMD/2004, date 2-12-2008

Sir.

Sub:- Public Servants -- M.A.&U.D.Department-- Allegations of corruption against Sri Mamidi Janardhan, former Junior Assistant of Sadasivapet Municipality, Medak district -- Trapped on 9-2-2004--convicted--Crl.Appeal No.1345/2008 filed by A.O. in the A.P.High Court--Admitted--Intimation-- Reg.

Ref: 1. This office final report of even No. dated 18-5-2004.

2. Govt. Memo. N. 3890/Vig. IV. 2/2004-2, dated 26-6-2004.

3.Lr.No.84/VC-D1/04-3, dated 21-9-2004 of VC,AP.,Hyderabad.

4. This office Lr. of even No. dated 20-1-2005.

5. This office Lr. of even No. dated 25-10-2008.

Please refer to the correspondence cited.

In continuation of this office letter 5^{th} cited, it is to inform that Sri Mamidi Janardhan, former Junior Assistant of Sadasivpet Municipality, Medak district filed Crl. Appeal No. 1345/2008 in the High Court of A.P. questioning the conviction and sentence in C.C. No. 42/2004 on the file of the Principal Special Judge for S.P.E.&A.C.B. cases, Hyderabad and the Hon'ble High Court admitted the said appeal on 10-11-2008 and suspended the sentence of imprisonment pending disposal of the Appeal by Order in Crl.M.P.No.2083 of 2008 10-11-2008. Copy of the said order is enclosed herewith.

As per the instructions of the Government, the Departments have to take action forthwith for dismissal of public servants convicted of corruption and criminal misconduct immediately upon such conviction without waiting for the disposal of the appeals filed by them in the courts.

Further, it is pertinent to mention here that the A.P.High Court has suspended the sentence of imprisonment only and the conviction of the Accused Officer stands good.

Hence, it is requested that Sri M. Janardhan, former Junior Assistant may be dismissed from service immediately on the ground of conduct which has led to his conviction on a criminal charge under Rule 25(1) of the A.P.C.S. (C.C&A) Rules, without any notice.

As per the amendment of Rule 25(1) to the A.P.C.S. (CC.&A) Rules, 1991 issued through G.O.Ms.No.240, G.A. (Ser.C) Department, dated 14-8-2003 it is not necessary to consult the A.P.P.S.C. before any orders are made under Clause (1) of this Rule.

Copy of the dismissal order of Accused Officer Sri M. Janardhan, former Junior Assistant may please be furnished to this Bureau for record.

Yours faithfully,

Encl: As above.

2/6

for Director General.

Anti-Corruption Bureau,

A.P., Hyderabad.

The Vigilance Commissioner, A.P. Vigilance Commission, Hyderabad (w.e).

The Commissioner & Director of Municipal Administration, A.P, Hyderabad.

(Lr.No.2772/2004-E3).

<div style="text-align: center;"><img src="https://pplines-online.bj.bcebos.com/deploy/official/paddleocr/pp-ocr-vl-16-online//2c864cdc-48aa-4947-a1c9-5fab8dd5b938/markdown_1/imgs/img_in_seal_box_2056_65_2864_738.jpg?authorization=bce-auth-v1%2FALTAKDN8mY5KlNI7zaRpLmOqrw%2F2026-06-04T10%3A16%3A21Z%2F-1%2F%2Fef1dc286df21ce1ddcff9776f4b42fa915b42c22a5b99eb4038caec296edad92" alt="Image" width="23%" />  <div style="text-align: center;"><img src="https://pplines-online.bj.bcebos.com/deploy/official/paddleocr/pp-ocr-vl-16-online//2c864cdc-48aa-4947-a1c9-5fab8dd5b938/markdown_1/imgs/img_in_seal_box_2056_65_2864_738.jpg?authorization=bce-auth-v1%2FALTAKDN8mY5KlNI7zaRpLmOqrw%2F2026-06-04T10%3A16%3A21Z%2F-1%2F%2Fef1dc286df21ce1ddcff9776f4b42fa915b42c22a5b99eb4038caec296edad92" alt="Image" width="23%" />  22 DEC 2008 Dated: 18-12-2008  </div>   </div>


GOVERNMENT OF ANDHRA PRADESH 22 DEC 2008

MUNICIPAL ADMINISTRATION AND URBAN DEVELOPMENT DEPARTMENT

Memo No.3890/Vig.IV.2/2004-7

Dated: 18-12-2008

Sub:- Public Servants - Municipal Administration Department - Sri Marri Janardhan, former Junior Assistant of Sadasivapet Municipality, Medak District trapped on 9.2.2004 - convicted by ACB Court, Hyderabad - Dismissal - Regarding

Ref:- 1. Proceedings of Regional Director- cum- Appellate Commissioner of Municipal Administration, Hyderabad ROC. No.34/2004/A2, dated 17-07-2004.

2. From the DG, ACB, Hyd.Lr.No.50/RCT-NMD/2004, Dt.25.10.2008.

3. From the DG, ACB, Hyd. Lr. No. 50/RCT-NMD/2004, Dt. 02.12.2008.

The attention of the Commissioner and Director of Municipal Administration,

Hyderabad is invited to the references cited. He is informed that the Principal

Special Judge for SPE & ACB Cases, Vijayawada pronounced judgment on 13-10.2008 in CC No.42 of 2004 convicting Sri Marri Janardhan, former Junior

Assistant of Sadasivapet Municipality, Medak District to undergo rigorous imprisonment for six months and to pay a fine of Rs.500/- and in default to suffer one month simple imprisonment for the offence under section 7 of Prevention of Corruption Act, 1988 and he further sentenced the Accused Officer to under rigorous imprisonment for 1 year and pay a fine of Rs.500/- under section 13 (2)

r/w (1)(d) of Prevention of Corruption Act, 1988 in default of payment of fine to suffer one month simple imprisonment. Both the sentences of imprisonment shall run concurrently.

2. The Commissioner and Director of Municipal Administration, Hyderabad is also further informed that Sri Mamidi Janardhan, former Junior Assistant of Sadasivapet Municipality, Medak District filed Crl.Appeal No.1345/2008 in the High Court of AP questioning the conviction and sentence in CC No.42/2004 on the file of the Prl.Special Judge for SPE & ACB Cases, Hyderabad and the Hon'ble Court admitted the said appeal on 10.11.2008 and suspended the sentence of imprisonment pending disposal of the Appeal by order in Crl.M.P.No.2083 of 2008 dated 10.11.2008. Further, it is pertinent to mention that the A.P. High Court has suspended the sentence of imprisonment only and the conviction of the Accused Officer stand good.

3. The Commissioner and Director of Municipal Administration, Hyderabad is therefore advised to take immediate action for dismissal of Sri Marri Janardhan, former Junior Assistant of Sadasivapet Municipality, Medak District immediately on the ground of his conduct which has led to his conviction on a criminal charge under rule 25(i) of APCS(CCA) Rules, 1991 and also in terms of G.O.Ms.No.2, GA(Ser.C) Department dated 4.1.1999. The action taken may be intimated to Government.

4. The Commissioner and Director of Municipal Administration is also requested to file caveat petition in APAT/ High Court to prevent the Accused Officer from obtaining interim orders.

Dr.C.V.S.K.SARMA

PRINCIPAL SECRETARY TO GOVERNMENT

To

The Commissioner and Director of Municipal Administration, Hyderabad

The Director General, Anti Corruption Bureau, Hyderabad.

The Secretary, A.P. Vigilance Commission, Hyderabad

// forwarded by order //

Janardhan, Jr. Assistance Suspension from Service ned Disposed by Lower Filing of Leave  </div>   </div>


Sub:- Public Services - M.A.Deptt - Sadasivpet - Sri M.Janardhan, Jr.Assistant

Sadasivpet - Trapped by ACB on 09.09.2004 - Suspension from Service - Revocation of Suspension - Prosecution sanctioned - Disposed by Lower Court - A.O. Convicted - Dismissal of A.O from service & Filing of Caveat Petition - Reg.

Ref:- 1. Govt. Memo. No. 3890/Vig. IV. 2/2004-5, Dt. 14.11.2006.

2. PROGS.Roc.No.34/2004-A2, Dt.3011.2006 of the RDMA, Hyd.

3.Lr.No.50/RCT-NMD/2004, Dt.25.10.2008 of the DG, ACB, A.P., Hyderabad addressed to Government & copy marked to this office.

4. This office LR.Roc.No.2772/2004-E3, Dt.03.11.2008 addressed to the Government.

5. Govt. Memo. No. 3890/Vig. IV. 2/2004-7, Dt. 18.12.2008.

I invite your attention to the references cited. In the reference fifth cited (copy enclosed), Government have informed that, "the Principal Special Judge for SPE & AC B cases, Hyderabad pronounced judgement on 13.10.2008 in C.C.No.42/2004 (copy enclosed) convicting Sri M.Janardhan, former Junior Assistant of Sadasivpet Municipality, Medak District to undergo rigorous imprisonment for six months and to pay a fine of Rs.500/- and in default, to suffer one month, simple imprisonment for the offence under section 7 of Prevention of Corruption Act, 1988 and he further sentenced the Accused Officer to undergo rigorous imprisonment for 1 year and pay a fine of Rs.500/- under section 13 (2) r/w (1) (d) of Prevention of Corruption Act, 1988, in default of payment of fine, to suffer one month simple imprisonment. Both the sentences of imprisonment shall run concurrently".

2. Further, Government have informed that, "Sri M. Janardhan, former Junior Assistant of Sadasivpet Municipality, Medak District filed Crl. Appeal. No. 1345/2008 in the High Court of A.P. questioning the conviction and sentence in C.C. No. 42/2004 on the file of the Prl. Special Judge for SPE & ACB Cases, Hyderabad and the Hon'ble Court admitted the said appeal on 10.11.2008 and suspended the sentence of imprisonment pending disposal of the Appeal by order in Crl.M.O. No. 2083/2008 dated. 10.11.2008". Further informed that, "it is pertinent to mention that the A.P. High Court has suspended the sentence of imprisonment only and the conviction of the Accused Officer stand good". Therefore, Government have advised the Commissioner & Director of Municipal Administration to take immediate action for dismissal of Sri M. Janardhan, former Junior Assistant of Sadasivpet Municipality, Medak District immediately on the ground of his conduct which has led to his conviction on a criminal charge under Rule 25 (i) of Andhra Pradesh Civil Services (CC&A) Rules, 1991 and also in terms of G.O.Ms. No.2., G.A (Ser.C) Dept., Dt.04.01.1999 and intimate the action taken to Government. Further Government have also requested to file caveat petition in Andhra Pradesh Administrative Tribunal/ High Court to prevent the Accused Officer from obtaining interim orders.

<div style="text-align: center;"><img src="https://pplines-online.bj.bcebos.com/deploy/official/paddleocr/pp-ocr-vl-16-online//2c864cdc-48aa-4947-a1c9-5fab8dd5b938/markdown_2/imgs/img_in_image_box_265_4043_602_4883.jpg?authorization=bce-auth-v1%2FALTAKDN8mY5KlNI7zaRpLmOqrw%2F2026-06-04T10%3A16%3A26Z%2F-1%2F%2Ff8c02bf49d49a69abf652770b86bbae1491bac97f90a1f78e88c2442bd4400ea" alt="Image" width="9%" /></div>


3. In view of the above, I request to take immediate action for dismissal of Sri M. Janardhan, former Junior Assistant of Sadasivpet Municipality, Medak District immediately on the ground of his conduct which has led to his conviction on a criminal charge under Rule 25 (i) of Andhra Pradesh Civil Services (CC&A) Rules, 1991 and also in terms of G.O.Ms.No.2., G.A (Ser.C) Dept., Dt.04.01.1999, as per the power conferred under G.O.Ms.No.292 MA&UD (L-3), Dt.16.05.1992 and report compliance to this office

Aec

& Government. Further you are requested to file caveat petition in Andhra Pradesh Administrative Tribunal/ High Court to prevent the Accused Officer from obtaining interim orders and report compliance to this office & Government immediately.

This may be treated as MOST IMMEDIATE.

Encl:- As above.
